# List 8

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doff</b></td><td>/dɑːf/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>脱下</td><td>doff one's hat<br>脱帽致意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors were expected to doff their hats upon entering the small chapel.  参观者进入这座小礼拜堂时须脱帽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put off</td><td>/pʊt ˈɔːf/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>推迟，拖延</td><td>put off the meeting<br>推迟会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't put off the dentist appointment any longer than necessary.  牙医预约别再往后拖了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take off</td><td>/teɪk ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>脱掉；起飞</td><td>take off your coat<br>脱掉外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please take off your muddy boots before entering the house.  进屋前请脱掉沾泥的靴子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 don</td><td>/dɑːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>穿上</td><td>don a uniform<br>穿上制服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firefighters donned their heavy gear in less than one minute.  消防员不到一分钟就穿上了沉重的装备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 put on</td><td>/pʊt ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>穿上，戴上</td><td>put on a jacket<br>穿上夹克</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She put on her gloves before stepping into the snow.  她走进雪地前戴上了手套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dogged</b></td><td>/ˈdɔːɡɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽强的；固执的</td><td>dogged determination<br>顽强的决心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dogged determination carried the project through two years of setbacks.  她顽强的决心让这个项目度过了两年的挫折。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hardheaded</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdˌhedɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；务实精明的</td><td>a hardheaded negotiator<br>强硬的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hardheaded refusal to compromise stalled the talks for weeks.  她固执地拒绝妥协，使谈判停滞了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headstrong</td><td>/ˈhedˌstrɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，固执的</td><td>a headstrong child<br>任性的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headstrong young man ignored every warning from his parents.  这个任性的年轻人对父母的每句劝告都置之不理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflexible</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfleksəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不肯变通的，僵化的</td><td>an inflexible rule<br>不容变通的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was inflexible about the submission deadline.  经理在提交截止日期上毫不通融。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intransigent</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænzɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不妥协的，固执的</td><td>an intransigent attitude<br>不妥协的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides remained intransigent, so the negotiations collapsed.  双方都毫不妥协，谈判因此破裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 mulish</del></td><td>/ˈmjuːlɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的</td><td>mulish refusal to listen<br>固执地不肯听</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mulish refusal to listen frustrated everyone on the committee.  他固执地拒绝倾听，让委员会所有人都很沮丧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obdurate opinionated</td><td>/ˈɑbdərət əˈpɪnjəneɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽固而固执己见的</td><td>obdurate, opinionated critics<br>顽固自负的批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee split between obdurate, opinionated factions on every issue.  委员会在每个议题上都分裂为顽固而固执己见的派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ossified</td><td>/ˈɑːsəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>僵化的，守旧的；骨化的</td><td>an ossified bureaucracy<br>僵化的官僚体系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Decades of success left the firm ossified and slow to adapt.  数十年的成功使这家公司僵化，难以适应变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-opinionated</td><td>/ˌselfəˈpɪnjəneɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执己见的，自以为是的</td><td>a self-opinionated columnist<br>自以为是的专栏作家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is too self-opinionated to accept editorial advice.  他太自以为是，不肯接受编辑的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-willed</td><td>/ˌselfˈwɪld/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，固执的</td><td>a self-willed child<br>任性的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The self-willed teenager ignored every warning from her parents.  这个任性的少女无视父母的一切警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stubborn</td><td>/ˈstʌbərn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，顽固的</td><td>as stubborn as a mule<br>像骡子一样固执</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandfather was too stubborn to see a doctor.  我祖父太固执，不肯去看医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unbending</td><td>/ʌnˈbendɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不亲切的；不妥协的</td><td>an unbending attitude<br>不肯通融的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old teacher was unbending about rules and manners.  这位老教师在规矩和礼节上毫不通融。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncompromising</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːmprəmaɪzɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不妥协的，坚定的</td><td>an uncompromising stance<br>毫不妥协的立场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took an uncompromising stand against corruption in the department.  她对部门内的腐败采取了毫不妥协的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unrelenting</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈlentɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不松懈的，不屈不挠的</td><td>unrelenting pressure<br>持续不断的压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unrelenting effort finally forced the company to apologize.  她不懈的努力最终迫使公司道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unyielding</td><td>/ʌnˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不屈的，不让步的</td><td>unyielding opposition<br>毫不让步的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union remained unyielding on the question of wages.  工会在工资问题上依然毫不让步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 willful</td><td>/ˈwɪlfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，倔强的</td><td>willful disobedience<br>故意的违抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His willful refusal to ask for help delayed the repair by weeks.  他固执地拒绝求助，使维修推迟了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acquiescent</td><td>/ˌækwiˈesnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，默许的</td><td>an acquiescent audience<br>顺从的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too acquiescent to protest, the staff accepted the new rules.  员工太顺从，没有抗议就接受了新规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amenable</td><td>/əˈmiːnəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，服从的</td><td>amenable to suggestion<br>愿意接受建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new manager is amenable to any reasonable suggestion.  这位新经理对任何合理的建议都乐于接受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliant</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a compliant employee<br>顺从的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were unusually compliant during the long ceremony.  在漫长的仪式上，孩子们出奇地听话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 complying</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，遵从的</td><td>complying with the rules<br>遵从规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firms complying with the standard receive a tax rebate.  遵从该标准的企业可获得退税。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flexible</td><td>/ˈfleksəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵活的，可变的；易受影响的</td><td>a flexible schedule<br>灵活的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offers a flexible schedule to parents of young children.  公司为有幼儿的员工提供灵活的日程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliable</td><td>/ˈplaɪəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的；易受影响的，温顺的</td><td>pliable young minds<br>易受影响的年轻心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young minds are pliable and easily shaped by the adults around them.  年轻的心智易受影响，很容易被身边的成年人塑造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliant</td><td>/ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的；顺从的</td><td>a pliant assistant<br>顺从的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted a pliant assistant who would never question his decisions.  他想要一个顺从的助手，永远不会质疑他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relenting</td><td>/rɪˈlentɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>态度软化的，让步的</td><td>show a relenting attitude<br>表现出软化的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After hours of pleading, his relenting expression gave us hope.  在几小时的哀求之后，他态度软化的表情给了我们希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yielding</td><td>/ˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，易屈服的</td><td>a yielding personality<br>顺从的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her yielding nature made her an easy target for pushy salesmen.  她顺从的性格使她很容易被强势的推销员盯上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dogmatic</b></td><td>/dɔːɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>武断的，自以为是的</td><td>dogmatic assertions<br>武断的断言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is too dogmatic to consider any alternative explanation.  他太武断，不肯考虑任何别的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doctrinaire</td><td>/ˌdɑːktrəˈner/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>教条主义的，照本宣科的</td><td>a doctrinaire approach<br>教条主义的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His doctrinaire approach left no room for evidence that contradicted the theory.  他教条主义的做法容不下任何与理论相悖的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opinionated</td><td>/əˈpɪnjəˌneɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执己见的，自负的</td><td>loud and opinionated<br>高声而固执己见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is opinionated about politics and refuses to listen.  我叔叔在政治上固执己见，不肯倾听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 opinioned</del></td><td>/əˈpɪnjənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执己见的，有成见的</td><td>a strongly opinioned man<br>固执己见的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old scholar was opinioned but generous with students.  这位老学者固执己见，但对学生很宽厚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 latitudinarian</del></td><td>/ˌlætɪtudɪˈneriən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>宽容的，不拘教条的</td><td>a latitudinarian attitude<br>宽容不拘的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His latitudinarian views angered the stricter members of the church.  他宽容不拘的观点激怒了教会中较严格的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undoctrinaire</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɑktrɪˈner/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不教条的，务实的</td><td>an undoctrinaire economist<br>不教条的经济学家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her undoctrinaire style made compromise possible.  她不教条的风格使妥协成为可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undogmatic</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɔɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不武断的，态度开放的</td><td>an undogmatic teacher<br>不武断的老师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book offers an undogmatic account of the dispute.  该书对这场争论给出了不武断的叙述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doldrums</b></td><td>/ˈdoʊldrəmz/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>低迷，停滞</td><td>in the doldrums<br>处于低迷状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The housing market has been in the doldrums all year.  房地产市场整年都处于低迷状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abeyance</td><td>/əˈbeɪəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>中止，搁置</td><td>hold a plan in abeyance<br>暂时搁置计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project was held in abeyance until funding arrived.  项目被搁置，直到资金到位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dormancy</td><td>/ˈdɔːrmənsi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>休眠，静止状态</td><td>break dormancy<br>打破休眠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Seeds emerge from dormancy when the soil warms.  土壤变暖时种子便结束休眠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 latency</td><td>/ˈleɪtənsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>潜伏，潜在状态；延迟</td><td>a period of latency<br>一段潜伏期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The virus can remain in latency for many years.  这种病毒可以潜伏多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quiescence</td><td>/kwiˈesəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>静止，休止状态</td><td>a period of quiescence<br>一段静止期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano entered a long period of quiescence.  这座火山进入了长期的静止期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moratorium</td><td>/ˌmɔːrəˈtɔːriəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>暂停，延期执行</td><td>a moratorium on hunting<br>暂停狩猎的禁令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council imposed a moratorium on new building permits.  议会暂停发放新的建筑许可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 continuation</td><td>/kənˌtɪnjuˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>继续，延续</td><td>the continuation of talks<br>会谈的继续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The continuation of the project depends on further funding.  项目的继续取决于后续资金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dolorous</b></td><td>/ˈdoʊlərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧伤的</td><td>a dolorous sigh<br>忧伤的叹息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dolorous sigh escaped him whenever the subject of his hometown arose.  每次提到故乡，他都发出忧伤的叹息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agonized</td><td>/ˈæɡəˌnaɪzd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痛苦的，苦恼的</td><td>an agonized cry<br>痛苦的呼喊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an agonized look as the verdict was read.  宣读判决时她露出痛苦的神情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bemoaning</td><td>/bɪˈmoʊnɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>哀叹的，抱怨的</td><td>bemoaning his bad luck<br>哀叹自己的坏运气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the evening bemoaning the loss of his job.  他整晚都在哀叹失去工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bewailing</del></td><td>/bɪˈweɪlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>哀号的，痛惜的</td><td>bewailing their lost home<br>痛惜失去的家园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers stood bewailing the destruction of the temple.  村民们站着痛惜寺庙的毁坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deplorable</td><td>/dɪˈplɔːrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，恶劣的；可鄙的</td><td>deplorable working conditions<br>恶劣的工作条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors found deplorable working conditions inside the crowded garment factory.  检查人员在拥挤的服装厂内发现了恶劣的工作条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grievous</td><td>/ˈɡriːvəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严重的，令人痛苦的</td><td>a grievous mistake<br>严重的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report described grievous failures in patient care.  报告描述了病人护理中的严重失误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miserable</td><td>/ˈmɪzərəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痛苦的，凄惨的</td><td>feel utterly miserable<br>感到极其痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold rain made the whole camping trip miserable.  冰冷的雨让整个露营之行变得凄惨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plaintive</td><td>/ˈpleɪntɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>哀伤的，悲切的</td><td>a plaintive melody<br>哀伤的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A plaintive melody drifted from the open window of the old house.  一段哀伤的旋律从那座老房子敞开的窗子里飘出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rueful</td><td>/ˈruːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，懊悔的</td><td>a rueful smile<br>懊悔的苦笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He admitted his error with a rueful smile and a shrug.  他带着懊悔的苦笑和一个耸肩承认了错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorrowful</td><td>/ˈsɑːroʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，忧伤的</td><td>a sorrowful farewell<br>悲伤的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sorrowful eyes told us the news before she spoke.  她悲伤的眼睛在她开口前就告诉了我们消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 woeful</td><td>/ˈwoʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲惨的，糟糕的</td><td>a woeful performance<br>糟糕的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's woeful defending cost them the championship again.  球队糟糕的防守又一次让他们丢了冠军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 happy</td><td>/ˈhæpi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，幸福的</td><td>a happy ending<br>幸福的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were happy to see snow for the first time.  孩子们第一次见到雪很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 joyful</td><td>/ˈdʒɔɪfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，喜悦的</td><td>a joyful reunion<br>欢乐的团聚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village held a joyful celebration after the harvest.  收获后村里举行了欢乐的庆祝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jovial</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jubilant</td><td>/ˈdʒuːbɪlənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜悦的</td><td>a jubilant crowd<br>欢欣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nominee gave a short speech before the jubilant crowd.  被提名人在欢欣鼓舞的人群前发表了简短讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dolt</b></td><td>/doʊlt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>call him a dolt<br>骂他是笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not call him a dolt simply because he asked an obvious question.  别只因为他问了个显而易见的问题就骂他笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idiot</td><td>/ˈɪdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>白痴，蠢人</td><td>act like an idiot<br>表现得像蠢人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only an idiot would drive that fast in fog.  只有蠢人才会在雾中开那么快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moron</td><td>/ˈmɔːˌrɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>蠢人，笨蛋</td><td>some moron on the road<br>路上某个蠢货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some moron parked across two spaces again.  又有某个蠢货横占了两个车位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simpleton</td><td>/ˈsɪmpltən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（缺乏常识的）笨蛋</td><td>treat him as a simpleton<br>把他当傻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They treated the new clerk as a simpleton until he corrected their figures.  他们把新来的职员当傻子，直到他改正了他们的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dullard</td><td>/ˈdʌlərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>a hopeless dullard<br>无可救药的笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher wrongly treated the quiet boy as a hopeless dullard.  老师错把这个安静的男孩当成无可救药的笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 illuminati</td><td>/ɪˌluːmɪˈnɑːti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>the illuminati of the age<br>当代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salon gathered the illuminati of eighteenth-century Paris.  这个沙龙聚集了十八世纪巴黎的智者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intellectual</td><td>/ˌɪntəˈlektʃuəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>知识分子；智力的</td><td>a public intellectual<br>公共知识分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a leading intellectual in the reform movement.  她成为改革运动中的主要知识分子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>domicile</b></td><td>/ˈdɑːmɪsaɪl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>住所；为…提供住处</td><td>domicile the refugees<br>为难民提供住处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city agreed to domicile the refugees in empty school buildings.  市政府同意把难民安置在空置的校舍里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accommodate</td><td>/əˈkɑːmədeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使适应；容纳；迁就</td><td>accommodate different needs<br>满足不同需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The schedule was changed to accommodate everyone's travel plans.  日程被改动以适应每个人的出行计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bestow</td><td>/bɪˈstoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>授予，给予；安置，留宿</td><td>bestow an honor on someone<br>授予某人荣誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king bestowed the title upon his loyal general.  国王把这个头衔授予他忠诚的将军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lodge</td><td>/ˈlɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>提供住宿，寄宿；提出（申诉）</td><td>lodge a complaint<br>提出申诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lodged the travelers in a barn for the night.  他们让旅人在谷仓过夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harbor</td><td>/ˈhɑːrbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>藏匿，庇护；心怀（怨恨）</td><td>harbor a deep grudge<br>心怀深深怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family was accused of harboring a fugitive for several months.  这家人被指控藏匿一名逃犯数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put up</td><td>/pʊt ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>共同策划；提供；张贴</td><td>put up a scheme<br>合谋策划一个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two brothers put up the whole story to fool their teacher.  两兄弟合伙编造了整个故事来骗老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 banish</td><td>/ˈbænɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驱逐出境</td><td>banish him from court<br>将他逐出宫廷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king decided to banish the poet from the court forever.  国王决定把这位诗人永远逐出宫廷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expel</td><td>/ɪkˈspel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驱逐；开除</td><td>expel a student<br>开除一名学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school expelled two students for cheating on the exam.  学校因考试作弊开除了两名学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dominant</b></td><td>/ˈdɑːmɪnənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>处于支配地位的；（基因）显性的</td><td>the dominant player<br>占主导地位的一方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dominant player in the market set prices that everyone else followed.  市场上占主导地位的那家定的价，其他人都跟着走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ascendant</td><td>/əˈsendənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有影响力的，主宰的</td><td>in the ascendant<br>处于上升地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reformers were ascendant in the party for nearly a decade.  改革派在党内近十年处于主宰地位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leading</td><td>/ˈliːdɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>主要的，领先的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a leading authority on ancient Chinese bronzes.  她是中国古代青铜器的顶尖权威。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outweighing</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈweɪɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>比…更重要，胜过</td><td>benefits outweighing the risks<br>益处胜过风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With savings outweighing the costs, the plan was approved.  由于节省的开支超过成本，该计划获得批准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paramount</td><td>/ˈpærəmaʊnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最重要的</td><td>of paramount importance<br>至为重要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Safety is paramount when workers handle these highly flammable chemicals.  工人处理这些极易燃的化学品时，安全最为重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prevalent</td><td>/ˈprevələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流行的，普遍的</td><td>a prevalent belief<br>普遍的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The belief that talent is innate remains surprisingly prevalent among teachers.  认为天赋是天生的这种看法在教师中仍出奇地普遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 principal</td><td>/ˈprɪnsəpəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>主要的，首要的；校长</td><td>the principal cause<br>首要原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourism is the principal source of income on the island.  旅游业是这个岛上的主要收入来源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supreme</td><td>/səˈpriːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>最高的，至上的</td><td>of supreme importance<br>极其重要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court holds supreme authority over all such disputes.  该法院对所有此类争议拥有最高裁判权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subordinate</td><td>/səˈbɔːrdənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>次要的，下级的；使服从</td><td>a subordinate role<br>次要的角色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to accept a subordinate role in the research project.  她不愿在研究项目中接受次要的角色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recessive</td><td>/rɪˈsesɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐性的；内敛的</td><td>a recessive gene<br>隐性基因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His recessive nature kept him silent at every noisy family gathering.  他内敛的性格使他在每次热闹的家庭聚会上都沉默不语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>don</b></td><td>/dɑːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>穿上</td><td>don a uniform<br>穿上制服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firefighters donned their heavy gear in less than one minute.  消防员不到一分钟就穿上了沉重的装备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assume</td><td>/əˈsuːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>就任，承担（职权）；假定</td><td>assume office<br>就职上任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She will assume office as director next Monday.  她将于下周一就任主任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wear</td><td>/ˈwer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忍受，容忍；穿戴</td><td>will not wear that excuse<br>不会容忍那种借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager would not wear such a feeble excuse again.  经理不会再容忍这种苍白的借口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put on</td><td>/pʊt ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>穿上，戴上</td><td>put on a jacket<br>穿上夹克</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She put on her gloves before stepping into the snow.  她走进雪地前戴上了手套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doff</td><td>/dɑːf/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>脱下</td><td>doff one's hat<br>脱帽致意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors were expected to doff their hats upon entering the small chapel.  参观者进入这座小礼拜堂时须脱帽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>donor</b></td><td>/ˈdoʊnər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>捐赠人；供体</td><td>an anonymous donor<br>匿名捐赠者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An anonymous donor paid for the entire wing of the children's hospital.  一位匿名捐赠者出资建起了儿童医院的整个侧楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 donator</td><td>/ˈdoʊneɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>捐赠者，赠与人</td><td>a generous donator<br>慷慨的捐赠者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plaque lists every donator to the new library.  牌匾上列出了新图书馆的每位捐赠者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presenter</td><td>/prɪˈzentər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赠予者，颁发者；主持人</td><td>the presenter of the award<br>颁奖者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The presenter handed the medal to the young winner.  颁奖者把奖牌交给年轻的获奖者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contributor</td><td>/kənˈtrɪbjətər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>捐助者；投稿人</td><td>a major contributor<br>主要捐助者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was the largest contributor to the relief fund.  她是救灾基金最大的捐助者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subscriber</td><td>/səbˈskraɪbər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>捐款人；订阅者</td><td>a regular subscriber<br>长期订阅者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every subscriber to the fund received a printed report.  每位向基金捐款的人都收到一份印刷报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patron</td><td>/ˈpeɪtrən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>资助人，赞助者；老顾客</td><td>a patron of the arts<br>艺术赞助人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The duke served as patron to several struggling painters.  这位公爵资助了几位困顿的画家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sugar daddy</td><td>/ˈʃʊɡər ˈdædi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>供养年轻情人的富有男子</td><td>look for a sugar daddy<br>找个阔佬供养</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tabloids claimed the actress had found a wealthy sugar daddy.  小报声称这位女演员找到了一个有钱的阔佬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acceptor</td><td>/əkˈseptər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>受体，接受者</td><td>an electron acceptor<br>电子受体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In this reaction the molecule acts as an electron acceptor.  在此反应中该分子充当电子受体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doodle</b></td><td>/ˈduːdl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（无目的地）乱涂乱画；漫无目的地打发时光</td><td>doodle in the margins<br>在页边乱涂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He would doodle in the margins of his notebook during long lectures.  上长课时他常在笔记本页边乱涂乱画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess around</td><td>/mɛs əˈraʊnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>闲混，不认真对待</td><td>mess around all day<br>整天闲混</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop messing around and finish your report before midnight.  别再闲混了，午夜前把报告写完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dawdle</td><td>/ˈdɔːdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>拖拖拉拉，磨蹭</td><td>dawdle on the way<br>路上拖拖拉拉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dawdled over her homework until it was nearly midnight.  她做作业拖拖拉拉，一直到近午夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifle</td><td>/ˈtraɪfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>轻视，玩弄；小事</td><td>trifle with her feelings<br>玩弄她的感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not trifle with her feelings just to amuse yourself.  不要为了取乐而玩弄她的感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dormant</b></td><td>/ˈdɔːrmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>静止的，不活跃的</td><td>a dormant volcano<br>休眠火山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The virus can stay dormant in the body for years.  这种病毒可在体内潜伏多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 latent</td><td>/ˈleɪtnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>潜在的，不活跃的</td><td>latent talent<br>潜在的才能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good teacher can discover latent talent in the quietest student.  好老师能在最安静的学生身上发现潜在的才能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lurking</td><td>/ˈlɜːrkɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>潜伏的，隐藏的</td><td>a lurking danger<br>潜伏的危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A lurking suspicion kept him from signing the contract.  一种潜藏的疑虑使他没有签合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abeyant</del></td><td>/əˈbeɪənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>暂时中止的，休止的</td><td>an abeyant claim<br>暂时中止的主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The title remained abeyant until an heir was found.  在找到继承人之前，该头衔一直处于中止状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quiescent</td><td>/kwiˈesnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，静止的</td><td>a quiescent period<br>静止期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has remained quiescent for more than two hundred years.  这座火山已经平静了两百多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inert</td><td>/ɪˈnɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（人）懒惰缺乏活力的；（物品）惰性的</td><td>an inert gas<br>惰性气体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Helium is an inert gas, so it rarely reacts with other elements.  氦是一种惰性气体，因此很少与其他元素发生反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 active</td><td>/ˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活跃的，活动的</td><td>an active volcano<br>活火山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stays active by swimming every morning.  她每天早上游泳以保持活跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 busy</td><td>/ˈbɪzi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忙碌的，繁忙的</td><td>a busy intersection<br>繁忙的路口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctors were too busy to answer any of our questions.  医生们太忙了，无法回答我们任何问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 operating</td><td>/ˈɑːpəreɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>运转中的，运营的</td><td>operating at full capacity<br>满负荷运转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plant has been operating without a break since March.  这家工厂自三月以来一直不停运转。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dour</b></td><td>/ˈdaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的</td><td>a dour expression<br>严厉的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard's dour expression discouraged anyone from asking him for directions.  警卫严厉的表情让人不敢向他问路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strict</td><td>/ˈstrɪkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，严厉的</td><td>strict rules<br>严格的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents were strict about homework and bedtime.  她父母对作业和睡觉时间很严格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exacting</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，苛求的；费时间花心思的</td><td>exacting quality standards<br>严格的质量标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The airline maintains exacting standards for every part it installs.  这家航空公司对安装的每个部件都保持严格的标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fierce</td><td>/ˈfɪrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶猛的；激烈的</td><td>fierce competition<br>激烈的竞争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two teams faced fierce competition for the final spot.  两队为最后一个名额展开激烈竞争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gruff</td><td>/ɡrʌf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>生硬粗暴的；声音低沉的</td><td>a gruff voice<br>低沉粗哑的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gruff old sailor pointed silently toward the harbor entrance.  一位声音低沉的老水手默默指向港口入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intimidating</td><td>/ɪnˈtɪmɪdeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人畏惧的，恐吓的</td><td>an intimidating silence<br>令人发怵的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The examiner's intimidating stare made the candidate forget everything.  主考官令人发怵的注视让考生忘了一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rough</td><td>/ˈrʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，粗暴的；艰苦的</td><td>a rough surface<br>粗糙的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road grew rough as we climbed into the hills.  我们爬上山时路变得坎坷不平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stark</td><td>/ˈstɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严酷的，荒凉的；鲜明的</td><td>a stark contrast<br>鲜明的对比</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph shows the stark landscape of the desert.  这张照片展现了沙漠荒凉的景观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stern</td><td>/ˈstɜːrn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的，严肃的</td><td>a stern warning<br>严厉的警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher gave the boys a stern warning about cheating.  老师就作弊问题严厉警告了那些男孩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gentle</td><td>/ˈdʒentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，轻柔的</td><td>a gentle voice<br>温柔的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the door a gentle push and stepped inside.  他轻轻推了一下门，走了进去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mild</td><td>/ˈmaɪld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，不严厉的</td><td>a mild punishment<br>轻微的处罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The winter here is unusually mild and rarely brings snow.  这里的冬天格外温和，很少下雪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tender</td><td>/ˈtendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>温柔的，心软同情的；正式提出</td><td>a tender heart<br>一颗温柔同情的心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a tender heart and cannot bear to see animals suffer.  她心地温软，见不得动物受苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>douse</b></td><td>/daʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扑灭（火或灯）；浇透</td><td>douse the flames<br>扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to douse the flames on the upper floors.  消防员整夜奋战，扑灭了高层的火焰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quench</td><td>/kwentʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解（渴）；熄灭</td><td>quench one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A glass of cold lemonade quickly quenched my desperate thirst.  一杯冰镇柠檬水很快解了我的焦渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extinguish</td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>熄灭</td><td>extinguish the fire<br>扑灭大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to extinguish the burning warehouse.  消防员整夜工作扑灭燃烧的仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put out</td><td>/ˈpʊt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使不便，麻烦；扑灭</td><td>put you out<br>麻烦到你</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope my early arrival did not put you out too much.  希望我来得早没太麻烦你。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kindle</td><td>/ˈkɪndl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃</td><td>kindle a fire<br>点燃火堆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single spark can kindle a dangerous forest fire.  一颗火星就能点燃危险的森林大火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignite</td><td>/ɪɡˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，使燃烧；激起（感情）</td><td>ignite public outrage<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict ignited public outrage across the entire country within a few hours.  判决在几小时内就激起了全国的公众愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>downplay</b></td><td>/ˌdaʊnˈpleɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻描淡写，不予重视</td><td>downplay the significance<br>淡化其重要性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He downplayed his own role in the company's success.  他淡化了自己在公司成功中的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 de-emphasize</td><td>/diˈɛmfəsaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不再强调，减轻重视</td><td>de-emphasize test scores<br>淡化考试分数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new policy de-emphasizes test scores in admissions decisions.  新政策在录取决定中不再强调考试分数。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neglect</td><td>/nəˈɡlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>忽视，疏忽；忽略，怠慢</td><td>neglect his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He neglected his duties for weeks and was finally dismissed.  他数周疏忽职责，最终被解雇了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 understate</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈsteɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻描淡写；低估</td><td>understate the risks<br>低估风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials understated the flood damage to avoid alarming the public.  官员们对洪灾损失轻描淡写，以免引起公众恐慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 play down</td><td>/pleɪ ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>轻描淡写，淡化</td><td>play down the risks<br>淡化风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials tried to play down the scale of the leak.  官员们试图淡化这次泄漏的规模。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 address</td><td>/əˈdres/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>正视，处理；演讲；地址</td><td>address the problem directly<br>直接处理问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report addresses the risks the company had ignored.  报告正视了公司此前忽视的风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emphasize</td><td>/ˈemfəˌsaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强调，着重</td><td>emphasize the importance of<br>强调……的重要性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She emphasized that the deadline could not be moved.  她强调截止日期不能改动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 underscore</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈskɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强调</td><td>underscore the importance<br>强调重要性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report underscores the urgent need for cleaner drinking water.  这份报告强调了对更清洁饮用水的迫切需求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doom</b></td><td>/duːm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使注定（失败或毁灭）</td><td>doom the project to failure<br>使项目注定失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shortage of funds doomed the project to failure before it began.  资金短缺使这个项目在开始之前就注定失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fate</td><td>/ˈfeɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>命运，宿命；注定</td><td>seal someone's fate<br>决定某人的命运</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark sealed his political fate.  一句不慎的话决定了他的政治命运。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foredoom</del></td><td>/fɔrˈdum/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定（失败）</td><td>foredoom the plan<br>使计划注定失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor funding foredoomed the expedition from the start.  资金不足从一开始就注定了这次探险的失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foreordain</del></td><td>/ˌfɔrɔrˈdeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定，天命预定</td><td>foreordain the outcome<br>预先注定结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theologians argued that God foreordains every event.  神学家们主张上帝预先注定每一件事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 predestine</del></td><td>/ˌpriːˈdestɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先注定</td><td>predestine his failure<br>注定他的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old prophecy seemed to predestine the prince to an early death.  那个古老的预言似乎注定了王子会早逝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predetermine</td><td>/ˌpriːdɪˈtɜːrmən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先决定，预先确定</td><td>predetermine the result<br>预先决定结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Genes do not entirely predetermine a person's health.  基因并不完全预先决定一个人的健康。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doyen</b></td><td>/ˈdɔɪən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>资深人士，元老</td><td>the doyen of critics<br>批评界的元老</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For thirty years he was the doyen of critics at that newspaper.  三十年来他一直是那家报纸批评界的元老。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authority</td><td>/əˈθɔːrəti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（某领域的）权威人士；管辖权，控制</td><td>an authority on ancient coins<br>古钱币方面的权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a recognized authority on ancient Greek coins.  她是古希腊钱币方面公认的权威人士。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expert</td><td>/ˈekspərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>专家；熟练的，专业的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called in an expert to examine the cracked foundation.  他们请来专家检查开裂的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 master</td><td>/ˈmæstər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>大师，名家；主人</td><td>a master of disguise<br>伪装大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a master of the craft could carve such fine details.  只有行家高手才能雕出这般精细的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maven</td><td>/ˈmeɪvn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专家</td><td>a marketing maven<br>营销专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine hired a marketing maven to explain why the campaign had failed.  杂志请来一位营销专家解释这次宣传为何失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veteran</td><td>/ˈvetərən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵；有丰富经验的人</td><td>a veteran journalist<br>资深记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A veteran journalist, she has covered four wars and six elections.  作为资深记者，她报道过四场战争和六次选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virtuoso</td><td>/ˌvɜːrtʃuˈoʊsoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>技艺大师，演奏名家；技艺精湛的</td><td>a violin virtuoso<br>小提琴演奏大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violin virtuoso played the difficult passage without a single mistake.  那位小提琴大师毫无差错地演奏了这段难曲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 layman</td><td>/ˈleɪmən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>外行，非专业人士</td><td>in layman's terms<br>用通俗的话说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Explain the diagnosis so a layman can understand it.  请解释诊断，让外行也能听懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doze</b></td><td>/doʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>小憩</td><td>doze off in class<br>上课打瞌睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dozed off in class and missed the announcement about the exam.  她上课打瞌睡，错过了关于考试的通知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 drowse</del></td><td>/draʊz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打瞌睡，昏昏欲睡</td><td>drowse in the sun<br>在阳光下打瞌睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old dog drowsed beside the warm stove.  老狗在温暖的炉子旁打瞌睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nap</td><td>/ˈnæp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小睡，打盹</td><td>take a short nap<br>小睡一会儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He naps for twenty minutes after lunch every day.  他每天午饭后小睡二十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wink</td><td>/ˈwɪŋk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>小睡片刻；眨眼</td><td>not sleep a wink<br>一点没睡着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I did not sleep a wink before the exam.  考试前我一点都没睡着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slumber</td><td>/ˈslʌmbər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>安睡，沉睡</td><td>fall into a deep slumber<br>陷入沉睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The baby slumbered peacefully while the storm raged outside the window.  窗外风雨交加，婴儿仍安然沉睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wake</td><td>/ˈweɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>醒来，唤醒</td><td>wake up early<br>早早醒来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise of traffic woke me before dawn.  车流的噪音在黎明前把我唤醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>draconian</b></td><td>/drəˈkoʊniən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极其残酷的；十分严厉的</td><td>draconian punishment<br>严酷的惩罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such draconian punishment for a minor offense shocked the entire village.  对轻微过错施以如此严酷的惩罚，震惊了全村。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rigid</td><td>/ˈrɪdʒəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，僵硬的</td><td>rigid discipline<br>严格的纪律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school enforces rigid rules about mobile phones.  学校对手机执行严格的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ironhanded</del></td><td>/ˌaɪərnˈhændɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>铁腕的，严酷的</td><td>ironhanded rule<br>铁腕统治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The governor kept order through ironhanded control of the press.  这位总督通过铁腕控制新闻来维持秩序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strict</td><td>/ˈstrɪkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，严厉的</td><td>strict rules<br>严格的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents were strict about homework and bedtime.  她父母对作业和睡觉时间很严格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stringent</td><td>/ˈstrɪndʒənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的</td><td>stringent safety standards<br>严格的安全标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory now follows stringent safety standards set by the government.  这家工厂现在遵守政府制定的严格安全标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mild</td><td>/ˈmaɪld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，不严厉的</td><td>a mild punishment<br>轻微的处罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The winter here is unusually mild and rarely brings snow.  这里的冬天格外温和，很少下雪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genial</td><td>/ˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼亲切的；温和宜人的</td><td>a genial host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our genial neighbor invited the whole street over for dinner.  我们和蔼可亲的邻居请整条街的人来吃晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lenient</td><td>/ˈliːniənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽大仁慈的</td><td>a lenient sentence<br>从轻的判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge handed down a lenient sentence because of the boy's age.  由于这个男孩年纪小，法官作出了从轻的判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>drawl</b></td><td>/drɔːl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>慢吞吞地说</td><td>speak in a slow drawl<br>慢吞吞地说话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered in a slow drawl that made every sentence sound lazy.  他慢吞吞地回答，每句话听起来都懒洋洋的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extend</td><td>/ɪkˈstend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>延长，扩展</td><td>extend a deadline<br>延长截止期限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They agreed to extend the deadline by two weeks.  他们同意把截止日期延长两周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lengthen</td><td>/ˈleŋθən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>变长，加长</td><td>lengthen a skirt<br>把裙子放长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The days lengthen noticeably after the spring equinox.  春分之后白天明显变长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prolong</td><td>/prəˈlɔːŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>延长，拖延</td><td>prolong someone's life<br>延长某人寿命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New treatments can prolong life by several years.  新疗法能把生命延长数年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 protract</del></td><td>/prəˈtrækt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>延长，拖长</td><td>protract the negotiations<br>拖长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless objections protracted the meeting into the evening.  无休止的反对把会议拖到了晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 draw out</td><td>/drɔ ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>拖长，拉长；引出（话语）</td><td>draw out the vowels<br>把元音拖长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drew out every word until the sentence seemed endless.  他把每个词都拖得很长，句子仿佛没完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 drivel</td><td>/ˈdrɪvl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胡言乱语</td><td>talk complete drivel<br>满口胡言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet talked complete drivel about curing serious illness with colored light.  那本小册子满口胡言，说用彩色光能治重病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gibber</td><td>/ˈdʒɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>语无伦次地快说</td><td>gibber with fright<br>吓得语无伦次</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was gibbering with terror and no one could understand him.  他吓得语无伦次，没人能听懂他说什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dreary</b></td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desolate</td><td>/ˈdesələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>荒凉的；孤苦凄凉的</td><td>a desolate landscape<br>荒凉的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abandoned village looked desolate under the grey winter sky.  废弃的村庄在灰暗冬空下显得荒凉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sullen</td><td>/ˈsʌlən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闷闷不乐的</td><td>a sullen silence<br>闷闷不乐的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sullen silence fell over the table when the topic was raised.  提到这个话题时，餐桌上陷入闷闷不乐的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monotone</td><td>/ˈmɑːnəˌtoʊn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>单调的声音；单调的</td><td>speak in a monotone<br>用单调的语调说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecturer read his notes in a flat monotone.  讲师用平板单调的语调念稿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pedestrian</td><td>/pəˈdestriən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸无奇的，令人厌倦的</td><td>a pedestrian style<br>平庸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reviewer called the plot pedestrian and the dialogue painfully flat.  评论者称其情节平庸，对话乏味得令人难受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 somber</td><td>/ˈsɑːmbər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，不愉快的</td><td>a somber mood<br>阴郁的情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A somber mood settled over the town after the funeral procession passed.  送葬队伍过后，全镇陷入阴郁的气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 jocund</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，高兴的</td><td>a jocund gathering<br>欢快的聚会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jocund crowd sang and danced until the last lantern went out.  欢快的人群唱歌跳舞，直到最后一盏灯笼熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>drench</b></td><td>/drentʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使湿透，浸透</td><td>drenched to the skin<br>浑身湿透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sudden storm drenched us to the skin before we reached shelter.  一场突来的暴雨在我们找到避处前就把我们浇得浑身湿透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bathe</td><td>/ˈbeɪð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>浸洗，沐浴；笼罩</td><td>bathe the wound<br>清洗伤口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Moonlight bathed the quiet valley in silver.  月光把寂静的山谷浸在银色中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deluge</td><td>/ˈdeljuːdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大暴雨；大量</td><td>a deluge of complaints<br>大量投诉涌来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The announcement brought a deluge of angry letters to the editor.  这项公告引来大量愤怒的读者来信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 douse</td><td>/daʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扑灭（火或灯）；浇透</td><td>douse the flames<br>扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to douse the flames on the upper floors.  消防员整夜奋战，扑灭了高层的火焰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soak</td><td>/soʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使…湿透</td><td>soak the beans overnight<br>把豆子浸泡一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soak the dried beans overnight before cooking them the next morning.  把干豆浸泡一夜，第二天早上再煮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 saturate</td><td>/ˈsætʃəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使饱和，浸透</td><td>saturate the sponge<br>使海绵吸饱水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain had saturated the field before the match even began.  比赛还没开始，大雨已浸透了球场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sodden</td><td>/ˈsɑːdn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>湿透的</td><td>sodden ground<br>湿透的地面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our boots sank into the sodden ground after three days of rain.  连下三天雨后，我们的靴子陷进湿透的地里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sop</td><td>/sɑːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>浸湿；吸干（液体）；安慰物</td><td>sop up the spilled milk<br>吸干洒出的牛奶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a towel to sop up the milk spilled on the floor.  他用毛巾吸干洒在地上的牛奶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wet</td><td>/ˈwet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>湿的，潮的；弄湿</td><td>soaking wet clothes<br>湿透的衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My shoes got wet crossing the flooded street.  我的鞋在穿过被淹的街道时湿了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dehydrate</td><td>/diːˈhaɪdreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>去除水分，使干燥；使失去活力</td><td>dehydrate fruit for storage<br>把水果脱水储存</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Campers dehydrate fruit and meat so the food lasts for weeks.  露营者把水果和肉脱水，好让食物保存数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 desiccate</del></td><td>/ˈdesɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使干燥，使脱水</td><td>desiccate the soil completely<br>使土壤完全干透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months without rain desiccated the fields and killed the young corn.  数月无雨使田地干透，幼小的玉米也枯死了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dry</td><td>/ˈdraɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干的，干燥的；使干燥</td><td>bone dry soil<br>干透的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hang the towels outside until they are completely dry.  把毛巾挂到外面直到完全干燥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>drivel</b></td><td>/ˈdrɪvl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胡言乱语</td><td>talk complete drivel<br>满口胡言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet talked complete drivel about curing serious illness with colored light.  那本小册子满口胡言，说用彩色光能治重病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abracadabra</td><td>/ˌæbrəkəˈdæbrə/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>咒语；胡言乱语</td><td>mutter abracadabra<br>念念咒语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician waved his wand and shouted abracadabra.  魔术师挥动魔杖，喊出咒语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonsense</td><td>/ˈnɑːnsens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>胡说，废话</td><td>talk nonsense<br>说废话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of what he wrote in that letter was pure nonsense.  他在那封信里写的大多是纯粹的胡话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prattle</td><td>/ˈprætəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闲扯，唠叨</td><td>prattle on for hours<br>唠叨好几个小时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She prattled happily about her garden while we drank our tea.  我们喝茶时她开心地闲扯她的花园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gabble</td><td>/ˈɡæbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急促而含糊地说</td><td>gabble away nervously<br>紧张地急促乱说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child gabbled excitedly about everything he had seen that day.  孩子兴奋地急促讲述那天见到的一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diminutive</b></td><td>/dɪˈmɪnjətɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的</td><td>a diminutive figure<br>矮小的身影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She noticed a diminutive figure standing quietly near the entrance.  她注意到一个小小的身影静静立在入口附近。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minuscule</td><td>/ˈmɪnəskjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的</td><td>a minuscule amount<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A minuscule crack in the pipe caused the entire basement to flood overnight.  管道上一道极小的裂缝一夜之间让整个地下室被淹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 small</td><td>/ˈsmɔːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>小的，微小的</td><td>a small apartment<br>小公寓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They live in a small house near the river.  他们住在河边一栋小房子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pocket</td><td>/ˈpɑːkət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>袖珍的，小型的；口袋</td><td>a pocket dictionary<br>袖珍词典</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She carried a pocket edition of the poems everywhere.  她随身带着一本袖珍版诗集。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smallish</td><td>/ˈsmɔːlɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>略小的，偏小的</td><td>a smallish room<br>略小的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rented a smallish office above the bakery.  他在面包店楼上租了一间略小的办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subnormal</td><td>/ˌsʌbˈnɔrməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>低于正常的，低于标准的</td><td>subnormal temperatures<br>低于正常的温度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient's subnormal body temperature worried the nurses.  病人低于正常的体温让护士们担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undersized</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈsaɪzd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尺寸偏小的，过小的</td><td>an undersized player<br>身材偏小的球员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The undersized puppy needed extra feeding for weeks.  这只体型偏小的小狗需要额外喂养数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 outsized</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌsaɪzd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>特大的，超乎寻常的</td><td>outsized influence<br>过大的影响力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tiny country has an outsized role in world trade.  这个小国在世界贸易中的作用超乎其体量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 oversized</td><td>/ˈoʊvərsaɪzd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过大的，超大号的</td><td>an oversized coat<br>过大的外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore an oversized sweater that hung to his knees.  他穿着一件垂到膝盖的超大毛衣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whopping</td><td>/ˈwɑːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>a whopping increase<br>巨幅增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film earned a whopping two billion dollars worldwide.  这部影片全球赚了惊人的二十亿美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>drollness</b></del></td><td>/ˈdroʊlnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>滑稽，诙谐</td><td>the drollness of his remarks<br>他言语的滑稽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drollness of his remarks kept the whole table laughing through dinner.  他言语的滑稽让整桌人整顿饭都在笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comedy</td><td>/ˈkɑːmədi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喜剧；滑稽，可笑之处</td><td>a romantic comedy<br>浪漫喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was unintended comedy in his solemn speech.  他严肃的演讲里有意外的滑稽感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comic</td><td>/ˈkɑːmɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>滑稽的，喜剧的</td><td>comic relief<br>喜剧性的调剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clumsy servant provides comic relief in the play.  笨拙的仆人在剧中起到喜剧调剂的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 funniness</td><td>/ˈfʌninəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>滑稽，好笑</td><td>the funniness of the sketch<br>小品的好笑之处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Much of the funniness comes from the actor's timing.  好笑之处多半来自演员的节奏把握。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 hilariousness</del></td><td>/hɪˈleriəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极其好笑，滑稽可笑</td><td>sheer hilariousness<br>十足的滑稽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hilariousness of the misunderstanding kept us laughing.  这场误会的滑稽让我们笑个不停。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 humorousness</del></td><td>/ˈhjumərəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幽默，诙谐</td><td>the humorousness of the essay<br>文章的幽默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the gentle humorousness of her novels.  评论家称赞她小说中温和的幽默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pathos</td><td>/ˈpeɪθɑs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>悲怆，感人的哀婉</td><td>a scene of great pathos<br>极具悲怆的场景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final letter gives the story real pathos.  最后那封信让这个故事真正带上悲怆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>drone</b></td><td>/droʊn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>低沉单调地说；嗡嗡地叫</td><td>drone on about details<br>唠唠叨叨讲细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker droned on for another twenty minutes.  演讲者又单调地讲了二十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hum</td><td>/ˈhʌm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嗡嗡作响；哼唱</td><td>hum a tune<br>哼一首曲子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refrigerator hums quietly all through the night.  冰箱整夜轻轻嗡嗡作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buzz</td><td>/ˈbʌz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嗡嗡叫；嘈杂声</td><td>buzz around the flowers<br>在花丛间嗡嗡飞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bees buzzed lazily above the clover.  蜜蜂在三叶草上懒懒地嗡嗡飞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumble</td><td>/ˈbʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说，杂乱无章地说</td><td>bumble through a speech<br>含糊地讲完演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bumbled through his answer and confused the whole audience.  他含糊其辞地答完，把全场听众都弄糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>drudgery</b></td><td>/ˈdrʌdʒəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>苦工；单调、卑贱或无趣的工作</td><td>the drudgery of housework<br>家务的苦累</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Machines have removed much of the drudgery of housework in modern homes.  机器已消除了现代家庭中大部分的家务苦累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 labor</td><td>/ˈleɪbər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>劳动，苦工；劳工</td><td>manual labor<br>体力劳动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of hard labor had bent his back.  多年的苦力劳动使他驼了背。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slavery</td><td>/ˈsleɪvəri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>苦役；奴隶制</td><td>wage slavery<br>工资奴役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The job felt like slavery with no rest and little pay.  这份工作像苦役，没有休息、报酬又低。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toil</td><td>/ˈtɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>辛苦劳作，苦干</td><td>toil in the fields<br>在田里劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers toiled from sunrise until dark during harvest.  收获时农民从日出劳作到天黑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 travail</td><td>/ˈtræveɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>辛苦劳动</td><td>years of travail<br>多年辛劳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After months of travail, the small team finally finished the bridge.  经过数月的辛苦劳作，这个小团队终于建成了这座桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grind</td><td>/ˈɡraɪnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>磨碎，磨光；苦干</td><td>grind the lens smooth<br>把镜片磨光滑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers ground the marble surface until it shone.  工人把大理石表面磨到发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sinecure</td><td>/ˈsaɪnɪkjʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>拿钱不干事的职务；闲差事</td><td>a comfortable sinecure<br>舒服的闲差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The post turned out to be a comfortable sinecure with almost no duties.  那个职位原来是个几乎无事可做的舒服闲差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dubious</b></td><td>/ˈduːbiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满不定性的，值得怀疑的</td><td>a dubious claim<br>可疑的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advertisement made a dubious claim that no serious scientist would support.  那则广告作出了一个没有严肃科学家会支持的可疑说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debatable</td><td>/dəˈbeɪtəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有争议的，可商议的</td><td>a debatable assumption<br>有争议的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether the policy helped the poor is debatable.  该政策是否帮助了穷人尚有争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doubtful</td><td>/ˈdaʊtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以确定的，可疑的</td><td>of doubtful value<br>价值可疑的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is doubtful whether the bridge can carry such loads.  这座桥能否承受如此重量还很难说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disputable</td><td>/dɪˈspjuːtəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有争议的，可质疑的</td><td>a disputable conclusion<br>可质疑的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His figures are accurate but his conclusion is disputable.  他的数字准确，但结论值得质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 problematic</td><td>/ˌprɑːbləˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有问题的，成问题的</td><td>a problematic assumption<br>有问题的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The data set is problematic because many records are missing.  该数据集有问题，因为许多记录缺失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 questionable</td><td>/ˈkwestʃənəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可疑的，靠不住的</td><td>questionable evidence<br>可疑的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The deal rested on questionable accounting practices.  这笔交易建立在可疑的会计做法上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shaky</td><td>/ˈʃeɪki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可靠的，不稳的</td><td>shaky reasoning<br>站不住脚的推理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theory rests on shaky historical evidence.  该理论建立在不可靠的历史证据上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 certain</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，无疑的</td><td>certain of the outcome<br>对结果有把握</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We are certain that the letter was never sent.  我们确信那封信从未寄出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incontestable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈtestəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可争辩的，不容置疑的</td><td>incontestable proof<br>无可争辩的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph offered incontestable evidence of the meeting.  这张照片提供了会面的无可争辩的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undeniable</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈnaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可否认的，明显的</td><td>undeniable talent<br>不可否认的才华</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her influence on modern dance is undeniable.  她对现代舞的影响不可否认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ductile</b></td><td>/ˈdʌktaɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有延展性的；易受影响的</td><td>ductile metal<br>有延展性的金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young recruits are ductile and quickly absorb the habits of the older soldiers.  新兵易受影响，很快就学到老兵的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pliant</td><td>/ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的；顺从的</td><td>a pliant assistant<br>顺从的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted a pliant assistant who would never question his decisions.  他想要一个顺从的助手，永远不会质疑他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yielding</td><td>/ˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，易屈服的</td><td>a yielding personality<br>顺从的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her yielding nature made her an easy target for pushy salesmen.  她顺从的性格使她很容易被强势的推销员盯上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supple</td><td>/ˈsʌpl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的</td><td>supple leather<br>柔软的皮革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gloves were made of supple leather that fit her hands perfectly.  这双手套用柔软的皮革制成，和她的手完全贴合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intractable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtræktəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难对付的，棘手的</td><td>an intractable problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poverty remains an intractable problem in many rural regions.  贫困在许多农村地区仍是棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refractory</td><td>/rɪˈfræktəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倔强的，不顺从的</td><td>a refractory pupil<br>不顺从的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One refractory pupil disrupted the lesson for the entire class.  一个不顺从的学生扰乱了全班的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obdurate</td><td>/ˈɑːbdərət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；冷酷无情的</td><td>remain obdurate<br>仍然固执不改</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The landlord was obdurate and refused to lower the rent.  房东顽固不化，拒绝降低房租。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dulcet</b></td><td>/ˈdʌlsɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，令人愉悦的</td><td>dulcet tones<br>悦耳的音调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dulcet tones calmed the restless children within a couple of minutes.  她悦耳的音调几分钟内就安抚了不安的孩子们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delightful</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，宜人的</td><td>a delightful surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a delightful evening by the lake.  我们在湖边度过了一个愉快的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 euphonic</del></td><td>/juˈfɑnɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，声音和谐的</td><td>euphonic verse<br>音韵悦耳的诗句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Italian strikes many listeners as an unusually euphonic language.  许多听者觉得意大利语格外悦耳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mellifluous</td><td>/meˈlɪfluəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）甜美流畅的</td><td>a mellifluous voice<br>甜美悦耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrator's mellifluous voice made even the dullest chapters pleasant to listen to.  叙述者优美悦耳的嗓音让最枯燥的章节也变得动听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tuneful</td><td>/ˈtuːnfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，曲调优美的</td><td>a tuneful melody<br>优美的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The musical is remembered for its tuneful songs.  这部音乐剧因旋律优美的歌曲而被记住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winsome</td><td>/ˈwɪnsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，漂亮的</td><td>a winsome smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her winsome smile made the shy children relax almost at once.  她迷人的微笑让害羞的孩子们几乎立刻放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cacophonous</td><td>/kəˈkɑfənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，不和谐的</td><td>a cacophonous racket<br>刺耳的喧闹声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra produced a cacophonous din while tuning up.  乐队调音时发出刺耳的嘈杂声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grating</td><td>/ˈɡreɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，令人不适的</td><td>a grating voice<br>刺耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The grating noise of the saw filled the workshop.  锯子刺耳的声音充满了作坊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dullard</b></td><td>/ˈdʌlərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>a hopeless dullard<br>无可救药的笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher wrongly treated the quiet boy as a hopeless dullard.  老师错把这个安静的男孩当成无可救药的笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moron</td><td>/ˈmɔːˌrɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>蠢人，笨蛋</td><td>some moron on the road<br>路上某个蠢货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some moron parked across two spaces again.  又有某个蠢货横占了两个车位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simpleton</td><td>/ˈsɪmpltən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（缺乏常识的）笨蛋</td><td>treat him as a simpleton<br>把他当傻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They treated the new clerk as a simpleton until he corrected their figures.  他们把新来的职员当傻子，直到他改正了他们的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idiot</td><td>/ˈɪdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>白痴，蠢人</td><td>act like an idiot<br>表现得像蠢人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only an idiot would drive that fast in fog.  只有蠢人才会在雾中开那么快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wit</td><td>/wɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>机智，智慧</td><td>a flash of wit<br>一闪的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quick wit saved the interview after the microphone failed.  麦克风出故障后，是她的机敏救了那场访谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dupe</b></td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 victim</td><td>/ˈvɪktəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>受害者，受骗者</td><td>the victim of a scam<br>骗局的受害者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became the victim of an elaborate phone scam.  他成了一个精心设计的电话骗局的受害者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gull</td><td>/ɡʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>gull the unwary<br>欺骗粗心的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler gulled dozens of elderly investors out of their savings.  骗子欺骗了数十位老年投资者，骗走他们的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bamboozle</td><td>/bæmˈbuzəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗</td><td>bamboozle the customers<br>欺骗顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman bamboozled them into buying a worthless extended warranty.  推销员骗他们买了毫无价值的延保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheat</td><td>/ˈtʃiːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，作弊；骗子</td><td>cheat on an exam<br>考试作弊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cheated the old couple out of their entire life savings.  他骗走了这对老夫妇一生的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cozen</td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoax</td><td>/hoʊks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>an elaborate hoax<br>精心设计的骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph turned out to be an elaborate hoax created by students.  那张照片原来是学生们精心设计的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoodwink</td><td>/ˈhʊdwɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>hoodwink the voters<br>欺骗选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hoodwinked customers with promises it could never possibly keep.  这家公司用根本无法兑现的承诺欺骗顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 connoisseur</td><td>/ˌkɑːnəˈsɜːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鉴赏家（尤指艺术领域）；专家</td><td>a connoisseur of fine wine<br>美酒鉴赏家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a connoisseur of fine wine and rare books.  他是美酒和珍本书的鉴赏家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>duplicitous</b></td><td>/duːˈplɪsɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>两面派的，奸诈的</td><td>duplicitous behavior<br>两面派的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His duplicitous behavior toward both partners eventually destroyed the company.  他对两位合伙人的两面派行为最终毁了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crooked</td><td>/ˈkrʊkəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>弯曲的；歪的；不诚实的</td><td>a crooked country road<br>弯曲的乡间小路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old painting hung crooked above the fireplace for years.  那幅旧画歪歪地挂在壁炉上方多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceptive</td><td>/dɪˈseptɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺骗性的，误导的</td><td>deceptive advertising<br>欺骗性广告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The calm surface of the river is deceptive and dangerous.  河面的平静具有欺骗性，很危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonest</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，欺骗的</td><td>a dishonest business practice<br>不诚实的商业做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company used dishonest advertising to attract careless customers.  公司用不诚实的广告吸引不细心的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fraudulent</td><td>/ˈfrɔːdʒələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺诈的</td><td>fraudulent claims<br>欺诈性的索赔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The insurer refused to pay several obviously fraudulent claims last year.  去年保险公司拒付了几笔明显欺诈的索赔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 guileful</del></td><td>/ˈɡaɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，欺骗的</td><td>a guileful negotiator<br>狡诈的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guileful merchant tricked several villagers out of their savings.  这个狡诈的商人骗走了几位村民的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shifty</td><td>/ˈʃɪfti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（显得）狡诈的，（显得）欺诈的</td><td>shifty eyes<br>游移闪躲的眼神</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman had shifty eyes that made every customer uneasy.  那推销员眼神游移闪躲，让每个顾客都不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underhanded</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈhændɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，暗中进行的</td><td>underhanded tactics<br>暗中使用的手段</td></tr>
